<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt" align=center>#</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:12pt"><BR></P>
<BR>
<P style="page-break-before:always; margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; line-height:14pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:12pt"><B>ITEM 1A.</B></P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:3pt; text-indent:72pt; line-height:14pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:12pt"><B>RISK FACTORS .
</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">In addition to the other information in this Annual Report on Form 10-K, stockholders or prospective investors should carefully consider the following risk factors:</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>Our business is concentrated in and dependent upon the continued growth and welfare of the &#147;Michiana&#148; area that includes parts of northern Indiana and southwestern Michigan.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">We operate primarily in the &#147;Michiana&#148; area of northern Indiana and southwestern Michigan that includes the Indiana communities of Mishawaka, South Bend, Notre Dame, Granger, Elkhart, Goshen, Bristol, Middlebury, Osceola, Nappanee, Wakarusa, Michigan City, and LaPorte as well as certain Michigan communities, including Niles, Edwardsburg, and Cassopolis. &nbsp;As a result, our financial condition, results of operations and cash flows are subject to changes in the economic conditions in this area. &nbsp;Our success depends upon the business activity, population, income levels, deposits and real estate activity in these markets. &nbsp;Although our customers&#146; business and financial interests may extend well beyond our market area, adverse economic conditions that affect our specific market area could reduce our growth rate, affect the ability of our customers to repay their loans to us and generally affect our financial condition and results of operations. &nbsp;Because of our geographic concentration, we are less able than other regional or national financial institutions to diversify our credit risks across multiple markets. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>We may experience difficulties in managing our growth and our growth strategy involves risks that may negatively impact our net income.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">We have pursued and continue to pursue an internal growth strategy, the success of which will depend primarily upon generating an increasing level of loans and deposits at acceptable risk levels without corresponding increases in non-interest expenses. Our growth strategy has been to operate out of a limited number of locations. While no plans currently exist, we may establish new branches and product areas or
expand into new markets which may require significant upfront investments in technology, personnel and site locations. There can be no assurance that we will be successful in continuing our growth strategy and in continuing to improve or maintain our net income by leveraging our non-interest expenses.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">In addition to our internal growth strategy, we may expand into additional communities or attempt to strengthen our position in our current markets by undertaking additional branch openings. &nbsp;We believe that it generally takes several years for new banking facilities to first achieve operational profitability, due to the impact of organization and overhead expenses and the start-up phase of generating loans and deposits. &nbsp;To the extent that we undertake additional branch openings, we are likely to continue to experience the effects of higher operating expenses relative to operating income from the new operations, which may have an adverse effect on our levels of reported net income, return on average equity ,
 and return on average assets.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>We face intense competition in all phases of our business from other banks and financial institutions.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">The banking and financial services business in our market is highly competitive. &nbsp;Our competitors include large regional banks, local community banks, savings and loan associations, securities and brokerage companies, mortgage companies, insurance companies, finance companies, money market mutual funds, credit unions and other non-bank financial service providers. Increased competition in our market may result in a decrease in the amounts of our loans and deposits, reduced spreads between loan rates and deposit rates or loan terms that are more favorable to the borrower. &nbsp;Any of these results could have a material adverse effect on our ability to grow and remain profitable. &nbsp;If increased competition causes us to significantly discount the interest rates we offer on loans or increase the amount we pay on deposits, our net interest income could be adversely impacted. &nbsp;If increased competition causes us to relax<B> </B>our underwriting standards, we could be exposed to higher losses from lending activities. &nbsp;Additionally, many of our competitors are much larger in total assets and capitalization, have greater access to capital markets and offer a broader range of financial services than we can offer.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>Interest rates and other conditions impact our results of operations.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">Our profitability is in part a function of the spread between the interest rates earned on investments and loans and the interest rates paid on deposits and other interest-bearing liabilities. &nbsp;Like most banking institutions, our net interest spread and margin will be affected by general economic conditions and other factors, including fiscal and monetary policies of the federal government, that influence market interest rates and our ability to respond to changes in such rates. &nbsp;At any given time, our assets and liabilities will be such that they are affected differently by a given change in interest rates. &nbsp;As a result, an increase or decrease in rates, the length of loan terms or the mix of adjustable and fixed rate loans in our portfolio could have a positive or negative effect on our net income, capital and liquidity. &nbsp;We measure interest rate risk under various rate scenarios and using specific criteria and assumptions. &nbsp;A summary of this process, along with the results of our net interest income simulations is presented at &nbsp;&#147;Quantitative and Qualitative Disclosures About Market Risk&#148; included under Item 7A of Part II of this Annual Report on
Form 10-K. &nbsp;Although we believe our current level of interest rate sensitivity is reasonable and effectively managed, significant fluctuations in interest rates may have an adverse effect on our business, financial condition and results of operations.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>We must effectively manage our credit risk.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">There are risks inherent in making any loan, including risks inherent in dealing with individual borrowers, risks of nonpayment, risks resulting from uncertainties as to the future value of collateral and risks resulting from changes in economic and industry conditions. &nbsp;We attempt to minimize our credit risk through prudent loan application approval procedures, careful monitoring of the concentration of our loans within specific industries and periodic independent reviews of outstanding loans by our credit review department. &nbsp;However, we cannot assure you that such approval and monitoring procedures will reduce these credit risks.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">Our loan portfolio is focused on commercial lending, with the largest portion of our commercial loans concentrated in loans to manufacturers, building contractors, developers and business service companies. &nbsp;The risk of nonpayment of loans is inherent in all commercial lending, and if it occurs, may have a material adverse effect on our earnings. &nbsp;To reduce this risk, the commercial loans we make are normally secured by the borrower&#146;s accounts receivable, inventory, equipment and real estate. &nbsp;Nevertheless, our focus on small- to medium-sized businesses may result in a larger concentration of loans that have a higher risk of default than loans to larger businesses. &nbsp;We believe we can manage this credit risk through prudent lending practices and monitoring our loan concentrations within specific industries; nevertheless, we cannot assure that such monitoring and procedures will eliminate or even reduce such lending risks.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">For a complete discussion of our lending activities, see Item
1 of Part
1 of this Annual Report on Form 10-K.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>Our allowance for loan losses may prove to be insufficient to absorb potential losses in our loan portfolio.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">We established our allowance for loan losses in consultation with management and maintain it at a level considered adequate by management to absorb probable loan losses that are inherent to
our loan portfolio. &nbsp;The amount of future loan losses is susceptible to changes in economic, operating and other conditions, including changes in interest rates, which may be beyond our control, and such losses may exceed current estimates. &nbsp;At December&nbsp;31, 2005, our allowance for loan losses as a percentage of total loans was 1.06% and as a percentage of total non-performing loans was approximately 0.00%. &nbsp;Although management believes that the allowance for loan losses is adequate to absorb losses on any existing loans that may become uncollectible, we cannot predict loan losses with certainty, and we cannot assure you that our allowance for loan losses will prove sufficient to cover actual loan losses in the future. &nbsp;Additionally, because we do not have a history of any loan charge-offs, management&#146;s estimate is based on a mix of management&#146;s judgment and reserves
used by our peer institution s
. &nbsp;If our loan losses exceed our reserves, it may have an adverse affect on
our business, financial condition and results of operations.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>Our continued pace of growth may require us to raise additional capital in the future, but that capital may not be available when it is needed.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">We are required by federal and state regulatory authorities to maintain adequate levels of capital to support our operations. &nbsp;We anticipate that our existing capital resources will satisfy our capital requirements for the foreseeable future. &nbsp;However, we may at some point need to raise additional capital to support continued growth, both internally and externally. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">Our ability to raise additional capital, if needed, will depend on conditions in the capital markets at that time, which are outside our control, and on our financial performance. &nbsp;Accordingly, we cannot assure you of our ability to raise additional capital if needed on terms acceptable to us. &nbsp;If we cannot raise additional capital when needed, our ability to further expand our operations through internal growth and branch expansion could be materially impaired.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>Our community banking strategy relies heavily on our management team, and the unexpected loss of key managers may adversely affect our operations.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">Much of our success to date has been influenced strongly by our ability to attract and retain senior management experienced in banking and financial services as well as being
familiar with the communities in our market area. &nbsp;Our ability to retain executive officers, the current management teams, branch managers and loan officers of our operating subsidiaries will continue to be important to the successful implementation of our strategy. &nbsp;It is also critical, as we grow, to be able to attract and retain qualified additional management and loan officers with the appropriate level of experience and knowledge about our market area to implement our community-based operating strategy. &nbsp;The unexpected loss of services of any key management personnel, or the inability to recruit and retain qualified personnel in the future, could have an adverse effect on our business, financial condition and results of operations.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>Government regulation can result in limitations on our operations.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">We operate in a highly regulated environment and are subject to supervision and regulation by a number of governmental regulatory agencies, including the Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System, the Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, and the Indiana Department of Financial Institutions. &nbsp;Regulations adopted by these agencies, which are generally intended to provide protection for depositors and customers rather than for the benefit of stockholders, govern a comprehensive range of matters relating to ownership and control of our shares, our acquisition of other companies and businesses, permissible activities for us to engage in, maintenance of adequate capital levels and other aspects of our operations. These bank regulators possess broad authority to prevent or remedy unsafe or unsound practices or violations of law. The laws and regulations applicable to the banking industry could change at any time and we cannot predict the effects of these changes on our business and profitability. Increased regulation could increase our cost of compliance and adversely affect profitability. &nbsp;For example, new legislation or regulation may limit the manner in which we may conduct our business, including our ability to offer new products, obtain financing, attract deposits, make loans and achieve satisfactory interest spreads.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>We have a continuing need for technological change and we may not have the resources to effectively implement new technology.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">The financial services industry is undergoing rapid technological changes with frequent introductions of new technology-driven products and services. &nbsp;In addition to better serving customers, the effective use of technology increases efficiency and enables financial institutions to reduce costs. &nbsp;Our future success will depend in part upon our ability to address the needs of our customers by using technology to provide products and services that will satisfy customer demands for convenience as well as to create additional efficiencies in our operations as we continue to grow and expand our market area. &nbsp;Many of our larger competitors have substantially greater resources to invest in technological improvements. &nbsp;As a result, they may be able to offer additional or superior products to those that we will be able to offer, which would put us at a competitive disadvantage. &nbsp;Accordingly, we cannot provide you with assurance that we will be able to effectively implement new technology-driven products and services or be successful in marketing such products and services to our customers.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>There is a limited trading market for our common shares, and you may not be able to resell your shares at or above the price stockholders paid for them.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">Although our common shares are listed for trading on the Nasdaq
 Capital
Market, the trading in our common shares has substantially less liquidity than many other publicly traded companies. &nbsp;A public trading market having the desired characteristics of depth, liquidity and orderliness depends on the presence in the market of willing buyers and sellers of our common shares at any given time. &nbsp;This presence depends on the individual decisions of investors and general economic and market conditions over which we have no control. &nbsp;We cannot assure you that volume of trading in our common shares will increase in the future.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>System failure or breaches of our network security could subject us to increased operating costs as well as litigation and other liabilities.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">The computer systems and network infrastructure we use could be vulnerable to unforeseen problems. &nbsp;Our operations are dependent upon our ability to protect our computer equipment against damage from physical theft, fire, power loss, telecommunications failure or a similar catastrophic event, as well as from security breaches, denial of service attacks, viruses, worms and other disruptive problems caused by hackers. &nbsp;Any damage or failure that causes an interruption in our operations could have a material adverse effect on our financial condition and results of operations. &nbsp;Computer break-ins, phishing and other disruptions could also jeopardize the security of information stored in and transmitted through our computer systems and network infrastructure, which may result in significant liability to us and may cause existing and potential customers to refrain from doing business with us. &nbsp;Although we, with the help of third-party service providers, intend to continue to implement security technology and establish operational procedures to prevent such damage, there can be no assurance that these security measures will be successful. In addition, advances in computer capabilities, new discoveries in the field of cryptography or other developments could result in a compromise or breach of the algorithms we and our third-party service providers use to encrypt and protect customer transaction data. &nbsp;A failure of such security measures could have a material adverse effect on our financial condition and results of operations.<B><I> </I></B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>We are subject to certain operational risks, including, but not limited to, customer or employee fraud and data processing system failures and errors.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">Employee errors and misconduct could subject us to financial losses or regulatory sanctions and seriously harm our reputation. Misconduct by our employees could include hiding unauthorized activities from us, improper or unauthorized activities on behalf of our customers or improper use of confidential information. It is not always possible to prevent employee errors and misconduct, and the precautions we take to prevent and detect this activity may not be effective in all cases. Employee errors could also subject us to financial claims for negligence.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:9pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">We maintain a system of internal controls and insurance coverage to mitigate against operational risks, including data processing system failures and errors and customer or employee fraud. Should our internal controls fail to prevent or detect an occurrence, or if any resulting loss is not insured or exceeds applicable insurance limits, it could have a material adverse effect on our business, financial condition and results of operations. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>Failure to pay interest on our debt may adversely impact our ability to pay dividends.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">As of December 31, 2005, we had $8.0 million of junior subordination debentures that are held by a business trust that we control. &nbsp;Interest payments on the debentures total approximately $550,000 per year, which must be paid before we pay dividends on our capital stock, including our Common Stock. &nbsp;We have the right to defer interest payments on the debentures for up to 20 consecutive quarters. &nbsp;However, if we elect to defer interest payments, all deferred interest must be paid before we may pay dividends on our capital stock. &nbsp;Deferral of interest payments could also cause a decline in the market price of our Common Stock.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>Our loan portfolio has a large concentration of commercial real estate loans, which involve risks specific to real estate value.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">Real estate lending (including commercial, construction, and, to a much lesser extent, residential) is a large portion of our loan portfolio. These categories were $252.3 million, or approximately 74.5% of our total loan portfolio as of December 31, 2005. &nbsp;The market value of real estate can fluctuate significantly in a short period of time as a result of market conditions in the geographic area in which the real estate is located. Although a significant portion of such loans are secured by real estate as a secondary form of collateral, adverse developments affecting real estate values in one or more of our markets could increase the credit risk associated with our loan portfolio. &nbsp;&nbsp;Additionally, real estate lending typically involves higher loan principal amounts and the repayment of the loans generally is dependent, in large part, on sufficient income from the properties securing the loans to cover operating expenses and debt service. Economic events or governmental regulations outside of the control of the borrower or lender could negatively impact the future cash flow and market values of the affected properties. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">If the loans that are collateralized by real estate become troubled during a time when market conditions are declining or have declined, then we may not be able to realize the amount of security that we anticipated at the time of originating the loan, which could cause us to increase our provision for loan losses and adversely affect our operating results and financial condition. &nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>Our concentration of one- to four-family residential mortgage loans may result in lower yields and profitability. </B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">One- to four-family residential mortgage loans comprised $86.1 million, or 25.4%, of our loan and lease portfolio at December 31, 2005, and are secured primarily by properties located in the Michiana area. Our concentration of these loans results in lower yields and lower profitability for us. These loans are generally made on the basis of the borrower&#146;s ability to make repayments from his or her employment and the value of the property securing the loan.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><B>Our commercial construction loans are based upon estimates of costs and value associated with the complete project. &nbsp;These estimates may be inaccurate and we may be exposed to more losses on these projects than on other loans. &nbsp;</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:8pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">At December 31, 2005, commercial construction loans, including land acquisition and development, totaled $29.0 million, or 8.6%, of our total loan portfolio. Construction and land acquisition and development lending involve additional risks because funds are advanced upon the security of the project, which is of uncertain value prior to its completion. Because of the uncertainties inherent in estimating construction costs, as well as the market value of the completed project and the effects of governmental regulation of real property, it is relatively difficult to evaluate accurately the total funds required to complete a project and the related loan-to-value ratio. As a result, commercial construction loans often involve the disbursement of substantial funds with repayment dependent, in part, on the success of the ultimate project and the ability of the borrower to sell or lease the property, rather than the ability of the borrower or guarantor to repay principal and interest. If our appraisal of the value of the completed project proves to be overstated, we may have inadequate security for the repayment of the loan upon completion of construction of the project.</P>

<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; line-height:14pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:12pt"><B>ITEM 2.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:72pt; line-height:14pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:12pt"><B>DESCRIPTION OF PROPERTY.</B></P>
<P style="margin-top:11pt; margin-bottom:2.75pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"> T
he main office of the Bank and
 the Company&#146;s corporate headquarters is located at 3820 Edison Lakes Parkway, Mishawaka, Indiana. &nbsp;The premises consist of a 9,600 square foot, two-story brick building constructed in 1988 with parking for approximately 57 vehicles. &nbsp;The building is located on a major thoroughfare in Mishawaka, approximately two miles south of Interstate 80 and near the city&#146;s population<B> </B>center.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">The building has four interior teller stations and a night depository facility. &nbsp;Management believes that the building is adequately covered by insurance.</P>
<P style="margin-top:11pt; margin-bottom:2.75pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">The Bank began leasing the first floor of a building for its Elkhart Banking Center for approximately $99,000 per year starting in April of 2005. &nbsp;This facility is a full banking center and is located at 300 Junior Achievement Drive, Elkhart, Indiana. &nbsp;This location utilize s
 6,000 square feet of a three-story brick building constructed in 2005 with parking for approximately 30 vehicles. &nbsp;The building is located two blocks off of Main Street, within the downtown Elkhart River Walk Common&#146;s area, approximately eight miles south of Interstate 80 and near the city&#146;s population center.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">The facility has three interior teller stations and a night depository facility. &nbsp;Management believes that the facility is adequately covered by insurance.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">Management believes the facilities will be adequate to meet the Company and Bank needs for 2006. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">&nbsp;</P>
<P style="margin-top:12pt; margin-bottom:-14pt; line-height:14pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:12pt"><B>ITEM 3.</B></P>
<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:3pt; text-indent:72pt; line-height:14pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:12pt"><B>LEGAL PROCEEDINGS.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">As a depository of funds, the Company and/or Bank may occasionally be named as a defendant in lawsuits (such as garnishment proceedings) involving claims to the ownership of funds in particular accounts. &nbsp;Such litigation is incidental to the Company&#146;s business. </P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">Management is not aware of any pending litigation against the Company or the Bank.</P>
<P style="margin:0pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><BR></P>

<P style="margin-top:0pt; margin-bottom:3pt; text-indent:72pt; line-height:14pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:12pt"><B>SUBMISSION OF MATTERS TO A VOTE OF SECURITY HOLDERS.</B></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt"><BR></P>
<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:36pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:11pt">No matters were submitted during the fourth quarter of 2005 to a vote of the Company&#146;s stockholders.</P>

<P style="margin:0pt; text-indent:234pt; line-height:13pt; font-family:Times New Roman; font-size:12pt">#</P>
